### Think2SQL: Reinforce LLM Reasoning Capabilities for Text2SQL
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15077v1)
- **Authors**: Simone Papicchio, Simone Rossi, Luca Cagliero, Paolo Papotti
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have shown impressive capabilities in transforming natural language questions about relational databases into SQL queries. Despite recent improvements, small LLMs struggle to handle questions involving multiple tables and complex SQL patterns under a Zero-Shot Learning (ZSL) setting. Supervised Fine-Tuning (SFT) partially compensate the knowledge deficits in pretrained models but falls short while dealing with queries involving multi-hop reasoning. To bridge this gap, different LLM training strategies to reinforce reasoning capabilities have been proposed, ranging from leveraging a thinking process within ZSL, including reasoning traces in SFT, or adopt Reinforcement Learning (RL) strategies. However, the influence of reasoning on Text2SQL performance is still largely unexplored. This paper investigates to what extent LLM reasoning capabilities influence their Text2SQL performance on four benchmark datasets. To this end, it considers the following LLM settings: (1) ZSL, including general-purpose reasoning or not; (2) SFT, with and without task-specific reasoning traces; (3) RL, leveraging execution accuracy as primary reward function; (4) SFT+RL, i.e, a two-stage approach that combines SFT and RL. The results show that general-purpose reasoning under ZSL proves to be ineffective in tackling complex Text2SQL cases. Small LLMs benefit from SFT with reasoning much more than larger ones, bridging the gap of their (weaker) model pretraining. RL is generally beneficial across all tested models and datasets, particularly when SQL queries involve multi-hop reasoning and multiple tables. Small LLMs with SFT+RL excel on most complex datasets thanks to a strategic balance between generality of the reasoning process and optimization of the execution accuracy. Thanks to RL, the7B Qwen-Coder-2.5 model performs on par with 100+ Billion ones on the Bird dataset.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Think2SQL: Reinforce LLM Reasoning Capabilities for Text2SQL":

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the influence of reasoning capabilities on the Text2SQL task using Large Language Models (LLMs). It compares different training strategies, including Zero-Shot Learning (ZSL) with and without reasoning, Supervised Fine-Tuning (SFT) with and without task-specific reasoning traces, Reinforcement Learning (RL) optimizing execution accuracy, and a hybrid SFT+RL approach. The study examines how these strategies affect performance on the BIRD, SPIDER, Spider-Syn and Spider-DK datasets, evaluating models of various sizes and architectures, focusing on Qwen-Coder-2.5 variants. The main findings are that (1) task-specific reasoning traces significantly improve performance in SFT, especially for smaller models; (2) RL is effective across models, particularly for multi-hop reasoning; and (3) the SFT+RL combination offers strong generalization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper tackles a pertinent question: how does reasoning influence Text2SQL performance in LLMs? While existing works have explored reasoning in LLMs and Text2SQL individually, the paper provides a systematic comparison of various training strategies that specifically focus on incorporating and reinforcing reasoning capabilities, especially in the context of smaller LLMs. The incorporation of both synthetic reasoning traces for SFT and execution accuracy as a reward signal for RL is a valuable contribution. While the training strategies (SFT, RL, SFT+RL) are not entirely novel in themselves, their combination and in-depth analysis in the context of Text2SQL, with a specific focus on reasoning, adds novelty. The experimentation around the Spider-Syn and Spider-DK datasets to test generalizability further enhance the study's contributions.

**Significance:**

The findings have practical implications for the Text2SQL community. Identifying the benefits of task-specific reasoning during SFT and the effectiveness of RL in handling complex queries offers valuable insights for training more robust and accurate Text2SQL models.  The demonstration that smaller models (like Qwen-Coder-2.5 7B trained with RL) can achieve comparable performance to much larger models (over 100B parameters) highlights a crucial aspect for resource-constrained environments and promotes the development of efficient LLMs for database interaction. The ablation study of different components of the proposed methodology allows for better understanding and possible reuse by other researchers. The analysis of various training strategies' robustness over different datasets enables to provide specific recommendations based on the use case.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents a comprehensive evaluation of multiple LLM training strategies on diverse datasets.
*   **Clear Research Questions:**  The research questions are well-defined and directly addressed by the experimental results.
*   **Ablation Study:**  The ablation study provides valuable insights into the individual contributions of different components.
*   **Practical Implications:**  The findings offer practical guidance for training effective Text2SQL models, especially for resource-constrained settings.
*   **Reproducibility:** The explicit definition of hyperparameter settings and setup is positive for other researchers to be able to reproduce and iterate on the paper's insights.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Synthetic Reasoning Traces:** The use of synthetically generated reasoning traces raises concerns about their quality and potential biases. How well they align with real-world reasoning patterns needs to be validated further.
*   **Limited Scope of LLM Architectures:** Focusing primarily on the Qwen-Coder-2.5 family may limit the generalizability of the findings to other LLM architectures. It would be nice to evaluate the proposed techniques over a more heterogeneous set of LLMs.
*   **Simplified Schema Linking:** While isolating the SQL generation is beneficial for analysis, the simplified schema linking (only considering directly relevant tables) does not reflect real-world complexities.
*   **Binary Execution Accuracy:** The heavy reliance on binary execution accuracy as the primary reward for RL could be improved by rewarding partial query correctness for more efficient training.
*   **Lack of comparisons with SoTA Text2SQL methods:**  Comparing the proposed techniques with state-of-the-art methods is important for evaluating the advancements and the strengths/weaknesses of the proposed methods in contrast with the SoTA ones.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the Text2SQL field by systematically investigating the impact of reasoning on LLM performance and demonstrating effective training strategies. The findings regarding task-specific reasoning and RL offer practical guidance for developing more robust and accurate models, particularly when resource constraints are important. However, the reliance on synthetic reasoning traces, limited scope of LLM architectures, and simplified schema linking prevents the paper from achieving a higher score. These shortcomings need to be addressed in future research to solidify the paper's impact and applicability.


- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Generative Artificial Intelligence for Beamforming in Low-Altitude Economy
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15079v1)
- **Authors**: Geng Sun, Jia Qi, Chuang Zhang, Xuejie Liu, Jiacheng Wang, Dusit Niyato, Yuanwei Liu, Dong In Kim
- **Abstract**: The growth of low-altitude economy (LAE) has driven a rising demand for efficient and secure communication. However, conventional beamforming optimization techniques struggle in the complex LAE environments. In this context, generative artificial intelligence (GenAI) methods provide a promising solution. In this article, we first introduce the core concepts of LAE and the roles of beamforming in advanced communication technologies for LAE. We then examine their interrelation, followed by an analysis of the limitations of conventional beamforming methods. Next, we provide an overview of how GenAI methods enhance the process of beamforming, with a focus on its applications in LAE. Furthermore, we present a case study using a generative diffusion model (GDM)-based algorithm to enhance the performance of aerial collaborative beamforming-enabled remote secure communications in LAE and simulation results verified the effectiveness of the proposed algorithms. Finally, promising research opportunities are identified.
- **Summary**: Okay, I'll provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "Generative Artificial Intelligence for Beamforming in Low-Altitude Economy."

**Summary:**

The paper explores the application of Generative Artificial Intelligence (GenAI) models to beamforming techniques in the emerging low-altitude economy (LAE). It begins by outlining the characteristics and challenges of LAE, highlighting the need for efficient and secure communication. The paper then reviews conventional beamforming methods and their limitations in the complex LAE environment. The core of the paper lies in discussing how GenAI models, such as GANs, VAEs, GDMs, and Transformers, can enhance beamforming by addressing issues like scalability, real-time adaptation, multi-objective optimization, and antenna technology complexity.  A case study using a GDM-based algorithm for aerial collaborative beamforming in LAE is presented, showing improved security and energy efficiency through simulations.  The paper concludes by identifying future research directions, including semantic-driven beamforming and long-range wireless power transfer-driven beamforming.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Timeliness and Relevance:** The paper addresses a very relevant and emerging area – the low-altitude economy – and the challenges of communication in this context. LAE is receiving increasing attention, and applying AI, specifically GenAI, is a natural progression.
    *   **Comprehensive Overview:** The paper provides a good overview of the fundamental concepts of LAE, beamforming, and various GenAI models. It establishes the necessary background for understanding the potential benefits of GenAI in this specific application.
    *   **Addresses Key Challenges:** The paper identifies the key limitations of conventional beamforming methods in LAE scenarios and convincingly argues how GenAI can overcome these challenges.
    *   **Specific Examples and Case Study:** The inclusion of the GDMTD3 case study, although limited in scope, provides concrete evidence of the potential benefits of GenAI for collaborative beamforming. The simulation results are presented and analyzed.
    *   **Identifies Future Directions:** The paper proposes meaningful and insightful future research directions, such as semantic-driven beamforming and WPT-driven beamforming.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Depth in GenAI Application:** While the paper mentions various GenAI models, the discussion of their specific implementations and adaptations for beamforming in LAE remains somewhat high-level. More technical details on how these models are tailored to the unique challenges of LAE would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Case Study Scope:** The case study, while helpful, is somewhat narrow in its focus. It would be valuable to see more diverse and realistic LAE scenarios and a more comprehensive evaluation of the proposed algorithm against other advanced beamforming techniques (e.g., those incorporating traditional AI/ML, rather than only the 'baseline' algorithms).
    *   **Lack of Rigorous Comparison:** The performance comparison in the case study is limited. While the results show improvement with GDMTD3, a more rigorous statistical analysis and comparison with state-of-the-art beamforming algorithms (beyond the baseline methods) are needed to fully validate the effectiveness of the approach. The advantages of the GDM specifically need to be more explicitly demonstrated.
    *   **Limited novelty:** GenAI and ML more generally are increasingly applied to beamforming. While applying it to LAE is novel, the methods used are based on existing knowledge. The combination of the diffusion model within the DRL framework has been demonstrated previously, as has the application of reinforcement learning to beamforming. While the application to LAE is unique, the algorithm itself is an incremental extension of prior work.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    The paper makes a valuable contribution by highlighting the potential of GenAI for beamforming in LAE. While the individual GenAI models and beamforming techniques are not entirely novel, the *combination* of these technologies in the *specific context* of LAE and the explicit articulation of the challenges and opportunities are valuable. The insights into future research directions are also helpful. However, the work lacks a significant algorithmic or theoretical breakthrough that would establish it as a truly groundbreaking contribution.

**Justification for the Score:**

Given the strengths and weaknesses discussed above, I assign a score of **6**. While the paper is timely, relevant, and provides a good overview of the field, it lacks significant algorithmic novelty and a rigorous, comprehensive evaluation. The case study, while beneficial, is limited in scope and depth. The lack of in-depth discussion of the specific adaptations of GenAI models to LAE challenges further diminishes its overall impact. A score of 6 reflects that the paper makes a useful, incremental contribution to the field by highlighting a promising application area, but it does not represent a major advance in either GenAI or beamforming research. To achieve a higher score, the paper needs to provide more technical depth, a more comprehensive evaluation, and a more significant algorithmic contribution.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.NI
- **Score**: 6/10

### Empowering AI to Generate Better AI Code: Guided Generation of Deep Learning Projects with LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15080v1)
- **Authors**: Chen Xie, Mingsheng Jiao, Xiaodong Gu, Beijun Shen
- **Abstract**: While large language models (LLMs) have been widely applied to code generation, they struggle with generating entire deep learning projects, which are characterized by complex structures, longer functions, and stronger reliance on domain knowledge than general-purpose code. An open-domain LLM often lacks coherent contextual guidance and domain expertise for specific projects, making it challenging to produce complete code that fully meets user requirements. In this paper, we propose a novel planning-guided code generation method, DLCodeGen, tailored for generating deep learning projects. DLCodeGen predicts a structured solution plan, offering global guidance for LLMs to generate the project. The generated plan is then leveraged to retrieve semantically analogous code samples and subsequently abstract a code template. To effectively integrate these multiple retrieval-augmented techniques, a comparative learning mechanism is designed to generate the final code. We validate the effectiveness of our approach on a dataset we build for deep learning code generation. Experimental results demonstrate that DLCodeGen outperforms other baselines, achieving improvements of 9.7% in CodeBLEU and 3.6% in human evaluation metrics.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DLCodeGen, a novel planning-guided method for generating deep learning project code using large language models (LLMs). DLCodeGen first generates a structured solution plan, which acts as a global guidance for LLMs during code synthesis.  This plan is used to retrieve similar code samples (Code RAG) and abstract code templates (Template RAG). A comparative learning mechanism then integrates the outputs of Code RAG and Template RAG to produce the final code.  The approach is evaluated on a newly created benchmark, DLCodeEval, and demonstrates improvements over baseline methods in both automatic metrics (CodeBLEU) and human evaluations.  The paper also includes ablation studies and analysis of the impact of different components and temperature settings.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the specific combination of solution planning, dual retrieval-augmented generation (Code RAG and Template RAG), and a comparative learning mechanism tailored for deep learning code generation. While each of these techniques (planning, RAG, comparative approaches) has been explored individually in the broader context of code generation, the way they are combined and optimized for the unique characteristics of deep learning projects is relatively new.  The creation of a dedicated benchmark, DLCodeEval, is also a valuable contribution.  The concept of generating *plans* isn't entirely new (see Self-Planning), but the specific structure and use of the plan for retrieval and comparative learning distinguishes this work.  The abstraction into *templates* also seems novel and suited to the structure of DL code.
* **Significance:** The significance of the paper stems from its potential to address the challenges of generating complete and coherent deep learning projects with LLMs. Deep learning code is often complex, domain-specific, and requires longer code sequences than general-purpose code. The planning-guided approach provides a valuable framework for managing this complexity.  The empirical results show clear improvements over existing methods, suggesting that the approach is promising. However, there is a lack of comparison to the current best code generation models, such as Claude or newer GPT models. This weakens the impact of the experiments, as it is unclear if the technique will still perform well against better general code generation systems.
* **Strengths:**
    *   The structured solution plan is a good idea for providing global guidance and ensuring coherence.
    *   The dual RAG strategies (Code RAG and Template RAG) address different aspects of code generation (implementation details vs. architectural logic).
    *   The comparative learning mechanism effectively integrates the outputs of the two RAG strategies.
    *   The DLCodeEval benchmark is a valuable resource for future research.
    *   The paper provides a thorough experimental evaluation with ablation studies.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The choice of DeepSeek-V2.5 as the base model for code generation may limit the generalizability of the results. Using a more powerful and widely adopted LLM would strengthen the impact of the work. There is a secondary evaluation on GPT-4o-mini, but this is still not a state-of-the-art code generation LLM, limiting the impact of the work.
    *   The experiments could be enhanced by conducting A/B tests to independently verify the improvements stemming from specific components.
    *   While the performance of the plan predictor is assessed, the performance of the code retrieval component is not thoroughly assessed and discussed. If this component fails, it could have implications for the rest of the generation chain.
    *   The description of Template RAG could be more detailed. The process of extracting and generalizing from code samples is crucial, and more specifics are needed.
    *   Lack of a comprehensive failure case analysis of the generator. More discussion of when the models fail would be helpful.
    *   Lack of information on the sizes of the samples in the sample pool used for code RAG and template RAG.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by:
    *   Providing a new approach to deep learning code generation.
    *   Highlighting the importance of planning and retrieval-augmented generation.
    *   Providing a benchmark for evaluating deep learning code generation methods.
    *   Inspiring future research on improving the coherence and correctness of LLM-generated code.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a well-executed and innovative approach to deep learning code generation. The combination of planning, dual RAG, and comparative learning is novel and addresses specific challenges in this domain. The creation of DLCodeEval and thorough experimental evaluation further strengthens the contribution. However, the choice of base LLM and lack of comparison with more current models somewhat limits the significance of the empirical results. The lack of detailed information on the quality of the retrieval component and the template generation process weakens the contribution. For these reasons, the assigned score is:

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### Rethinking the Potential of Multimodality in Collaborative Problem Solving Diagnosis with Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15093v1)
- **Authors**: K. Wong, B. Wu, S. Bulathwela, M. Cukurova
- **Abstract**: Detecting collaborative and problem-solving behaviours from digital traces to interpret students' collaborative problem solving (CPS) competency is a long-term goal in the Artificial Intelligence in Education (AIEd) field. Although multimodal data and advanced models are argued to have the potential to detect complex CPS behaviours, empirical evidence on their value remains limited with some contrasting evidence. In this study, we investigated the potential of multimodal data to improve model performance in diagnosing 78 secondary school students' CPS subskills and indicators in authentic educational settings. In particular, text embeddings from verbal data and acoustic embeddings from audio data were used in a multimodal classification model for CPS diagnosis. Both unimodal and multimodal transformer-based models outperformed traditional models in detecting CPS classes. Although the inclusion of multimodality did not improve the performance of traditional unimodal models, its integration into transformer-based models demonstrated improved performance for diagnosing social-cognitive CPS classes compared to unimodal transformer-based models. Based on the results, the paper argues that multimodality and the selection of a particular modelling technique should not be taken for granted to achieve the best performance in the automated detection of every CPS subskill and indicator. Rather, their value is limited to certain types of CPS indicators, affected by the complexity of the labels, and dependent on the composition of indicators in the dataset. We conclude the paper by discussing the required nuance when considering the value of LLMs and multimodality in automated CPS diagnosis, highlighting the need for human-AI complementarity, and proposing the exploration of relevant model architectures and techniques to improve CPS diagnosis in authentic educational contexts.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the potential of multimodal data (text and audio features) and Large Language Models (LLMs) to improve the diagnosis of collaborative problem-solving (CPS) skills in secondary school students. The study compares traditional machine learning models with transformer-based models (BERT, AudiBERT) for detecting CPS subskills and indicators from audio data.  It aims to determine whether incorporating multimodality improves model performance compared to using unimodal data. The results suggest that transformer-based models generally outperform traditional models, and that the benefits of multimodality are dependent on the specific CPS indicator being detected and the dataset composition. The paper argues that multimodality and the choice of modelling technique shouldn't be taken for granted, and that a nuanced approach is required, highlighting the importance of human-AI complementarity in CPS diagnosis.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its empirical investigation of multimodality with *transformer-based* models for CPS diagnosis in an *authentic* educational setting. While prior research explored multimodal data for CPS, this study specifically examines the impact of text and audio features in LLMs and provides empirical evidence on the conditions under which multimodality is beneficial. This is somewhat novel given existing limited work leveraging LLMs to detect aspects of CPS and most relying on unimodal data.

*   **Significance:** The paper makes a significant contribution by challenging the assumption that multimodality always leads to improved performance. It empirically demonstrates that the value of multimodality is limited to certain types of CPS indicators, is affected by label complexity, and depends on dataset composition. This is important because it promotes a more critical and nuanced approach to using multimodal data in automated CPS diagnosis. The paper highlights the practical considerations for applying LLMs in real-world educational settings, which adds to its significance.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear research questions:** The study addresses well-defined research questions.
    *   **Rigorous methodology:** The paper uses established models and evaluation metrics.
    *   **Empirical evidence:** The findings are supported by empirical data.
    *   **Nuanced discussion:** The paper offers a thoughtful discussion of the results and their implications, avoiding overgeneralization.
    *   **Relevance to AIEd:** The study tackles a core challenge in the AIEd field.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited sample size:** The sample size (78 students) is relatively small, which may limit the generalizability of the findings, especially given the class imbalance.
    *   **Specific dataset:** The results may be specific to the design of the CPS task and the chosen set of CPS indicators.
    *   **Modest improvements:** While AudiBERT shows improved performance, the gains over BERT are modest. This could be because the selected audio features do not adequately complement the BERT model or that BERT already effectively captured relevant audio context from transcriptions.
    *   **Limited exploration of feature space:** The selection of audio features (just 11) seems relatively constrained. Exploring more diverse and potentially hand-crafted audio features could have generated greater improvements in recognition accuracy.

*   **Potential influence:** The paper's findings will encourage researchers to carefully consider the benefits and limitations of multimodality in automated CPS diagnosis. It highlights the need for human-AI complementarity and provides guidance on when and how to effectively leverage multimodal data with LLMs. It sets the stage for exploring more sophisticated model architectures and data augmentation techniques.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the AIEd field by providing empirical evidence that challenges common assumptions about the benefits of multimodality in CPS diagnosis with LLMs. While the improvements are modest and the sample size limits the generalizability of the findings, the paper's thoughtful discussion and practical implications make it a worthwhile contribution. The work encourages researchers to adopt a more critical and nuanced approach to using multimodal data in automated CPS diagnosis. In other words, the analysis, results and discussion surrounding the model differences are of interest for the field. Thus, the score is based on the insightfulness regarding the discussion and the empirical investigation in an authentic setting rather than the significance of the results itself.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### VistaDepth: Frequency Modulation With Bias Reweighting For Enhanced Long-Range Depth Estimation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15095v1)
- **Authors**: Mingxia Zhan, Li Zhang, XiaoMeng Chu, Beibei Wang
- **Abstract**: Monocular depth estimation (MDE) aims to predict per-pixel depth values from a single RGB image. Recent advancements have positioned diffusion models as effective MDE tools by framing the challenge as a conditional image generation task. Despite their progress, these methods often struggle with accurately reconstructing distant depths, due largely to the imbalanced distribution of depth values and an over-reliance on spatial-domain features. To overcome these limitations, we introduce VistaDepth, a novel framework that integrates adaptive frequency-domain feature enhancements with an adaptive weight-balancing mechanism into the diffusion process. Central to our approach is the Latent Frequency Modulation (LFM) module, which dynamically refines spectral responses in the latent feature space, thereby improving the preservation of structural details and reducing noisy artifacts. Furthermore, we implement an adaptive weighting strategy that modulates the diffusion loss in real-time, enhancing the model's sensitivity towards distant depth reconstruction. These innovations collectively result in superior depth perception performance across both distance and detail. Experimental evaluations confirm that VistaDepth achieves state-of-the-art performance among diffusion-based MDE techniques, particularly excelling in the accurate reconstruction of distant regions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces VistaDepth, a novel diffusion-based framework for monocular depth estimation (MDE) designed to improve the accuracy of long-range depth estimation. The method addresses two key limitations of existing diffusion-based MDE approaches: the imbalanced distribution of depth values in training data (the "long-tail" problem) and the over-reliance on spatial-domain features. VistaDepth incorporates a Latent Frequency Modulation (LFM) module, which enhances latent feature representations by refining spectral responses, and a BiasMap reweighting mechanism that adaptively balances training emphasis to prioritize distant and structurally complex regions. The authors demonstrate state-of-the-art performance among diffusion-based MDE techniques and improvements in accurate reconstruction of distant regions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper addresses a relevant and persistent problem in monocular depth estimation: the difficulty in accurately estimating depths for distant objects. While diffusion models have shown promise in MDE, their performance is often limited by the skewed distribution of depth values and a lack of focus on long-range dependencies. VistaDepth proposes two key innovations to address these limitations:

*   **Latent Frequency Modulation (LFM):** This module is designed to capture and refine the frequency components of latent features. The motivation is that distant regions are typically characterized by low-frequency signals that can be lost during the diffusion process. While the idea of incorporating frequency information is not entirely new in computer vision, its application within the latent space of a diffusion model for MDE is a novel contribution. However, the specific implementation of LFM (FFT, splitting into low/high frequencies, trainable cutoff) is relatively straightforward, but it is shown to be effective.

*   **BiasMap:** The adaptive reweighting mechanism is designed to address the long-tail distribution by prioritizing distant and structurally complex regions during training. The combination of edge-weight and distance-weight maps is a clever way to focus supervision on these areas.

**Strengths:**

*   **Problem Relevance:** Addresses a clear limitation in MDE.
*   **Novelty:** The combination of LFM and BiasMap within a diffusion framework is a novel approach to enhance long-range depth estimation. While each component isn't revolutionary, their synergy is noteworthy.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper includes a thorough experimental evaluation on multiple datasets, demonstrating state-of-the-art performance compared to other diffusion-based methods. The ablation studies are helpful in understanding the contribution of each component. Qualitative results are also provided, visually demonstrating the improved depth predictions.
*   **Zero-Shot Capability:** The emphasis on zero-shot performance strengthens the practicality of the method, indicating its robustness to domain shifts.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Technical Depth:** While the approach is effective, the technical novelty could be stronger. The LFM module, while useful, uses relatively standard frequency domain techniques. The BiasMap also is a relatively simple mechanism. A deeper theoretical analysis of why these components are effective in the latent space would be beneficial.
*   **Reliance on Pre-trained Models:**  The method relies on a pre-trained Stable Diffusion model. This is standard practice in this area and not a weakness per se, but it means that the improvements are relative to the capabilities of the pre-trained backbone.
*   **Qualitative Results:** Although the qualitative results are good, more comprehensive visualizations and comparisons to other methods on complex scenes would further strengthen the paper. Also showing the attention weights of the bias map could be very valuable.

**Significance:**

The paper contributes to the advancement of MDE by proposing a practical and effective solution for improving long-range depth estimation in diffusion-based frameworks. The proposed techniques are likely to be of interest to researchers and practitioners in the field of computer vision, particularly those working on applications that require accurate depth perception, such as autonomous driving and robotics.

**Justification for Score:**

The VistaDepth paper introduces a novel combination of techniques that effectively addresses the challenges of long-range depth estimation in diffusion-based MDE. The experimental results and ablation studies are compelling, demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed approach. While the individual components (frequency modulation, reweighting) are not groundbreaking in isolation, their synergistic application within the diffusion framework represents a significant contribution. The paper would have been improved with greater technical depth and more compelling qualitative evaluations. Given these points, the paper merits a score of **7**.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Fast-Slow Co-advancing Optimizer: Toward Harmonious Adversarial Training of GAN
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15099v1)
- **Authors**: Lin Wang, Xiancheng Wang, Rui Wang, Zhibo Zhang, Minghang Zhao
- **Abstract**: Up to now, the training processes of typical Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs) are still particularly sensitive to data properties and hyperparameters, which may lead to severe oscillations, difficulties in convergence, or even failures to converge, especially when the overall variances of the training sets are large. These phenomena are often attributed to the training characteristics of such networks. Aiming at the problem, this paper develops a new intelligent optimizer, Fast-Slow Co-advancing Optimizer (FSCO), which employs reinforcement learning in the training process of GANs to make training easier. Specifically, this paper allows the training step size to be controlled by an agent to improve training stability, and makes the training process more intelligent with variable learning rates, making GANs less sensitive to step size. Experiments have been conducted on three benchmark datasets to verify the effectiveness of the developed FSCO.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel optimizer, the Fast-Slow Co-advancing Optimizer (FSCO), designed to improve the training stability and reduce sensitivity to hyperparameters in Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs). FSCO employs reinforcement learning (specifically DDPG) to dynamically control the discriminator's step size, allowing it to adapt to the generator's learning progress. This adaptive control aims to balance the adversarial interaction, preventing mode collapse and promoting more harmonious training. The paper validates FSCO's effectiveness through experiments on MNIST, ANIME, and Ganyu datasets, demonstrating improved stability and reduced hyperparameter sensitivity compared to traditional GAN training approaches.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using reinforcement learning to adaptively control the discriminator's learning rate is somewhat novel in the context of GAN training. While RL-based optimizers exist, their direct application to balancing the adversarial dynamics between generator and discriminator is less explored. The FSCO framework presents a concrete implementation of this idea.

*   **Significance:** GAN training is notoriously unstable and hyperparameter-sensitive. Therefore, any method that offers improved stability and reduced tuning requirements is potentially significant. The experimental results suggest that FSCO achieves this to some extent. The importance lies in potentially making GANs more accessible and easier to deploy in various applications, particularly where large datasets and high resolution images are concerned. The contribution is towards better GAN training, but also an demonstration of the use of RL in a setting that has two models to coordinate, not just one.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem statement: The paper addresses a well-known and persistent challenge in GAN training.
    *   Novel approach: Using DDPG to control the discriminator's step size is a creative solution.
    *   Experimental validation: The paper includes experiments on multiple datasets to demonstrate FSCO's effectiveness.
    *   Detailed explanation: Algorithm’s steps are explained clearly, that makes the algorithm reproducible.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited empirical scope: The experimental setup, while demonstrating improvements, may not be comprehensive enough to showcase the method's advantage on different datasets. Further testing and comparisons should be performed. The hyperparameter tuning for FSCO itself still relies on manual labor and knowledge.
    *   Generality concerns: The paper states "FSCO cannot enhance the network's inherent capabilities", and its application to high resolution images failed, which suggest the method may require more tuning in terms of the ratio between fixed learning rates of discriminator and generator, and the DDPG network setup.
    *   Incomplete analysis: Further analysis needs to be done to analyse the effect of the exploration noise parameter. The effect of other RL algorithm choices must also be considered.

*   **Impact:** While the paper offers a promising approach, the impact will depend on its adoption and further development by the community. If FSCO can be shown to consistently improve GAN training across diverse datasets and architectures, it could become a valuable tool for researchers and practitioners. However, its limitations need to be addressed to realize its full potential. If the algorithm can be incorporated into other optimisation algorithm it will be a significant improvement.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** The score reflects a solid contribution to the GAN literature, but the limitations discussed prevent it from being considered exceptional. The novelty lies in the specific implementation and application of DDPG for this purpose, and its effectiveness is supported by experiments. The weaknesses, however, need to be addressed with more robust experimentation and analysis to improve robustness.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### Contemplative Wisdom for Superalignment
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15125v1)
- **Authors**: Ruben Laukkonen, Fionn Inglis, Shamil Chandaria, Lars Sandved-Smith, Jakob Hohwy, Jonathan Gold, Adam Elwood
- **Abstract**: As artificial intelligence (AI) improves, traditional alignment strategies may falter in the face of unpredictable self-improvement, hidden subgoals, and the sheer complexity of intelligent systems. Rather than externally constraining behavior, we advocate designing AI with intrinsic morality built into its cognitive architecture and world model. Inspired by contemplative wisdom traditions, we show how four axiomatic principles can instil a resilient Wise World Model in AI systems. First, mindfulness enables self-monitoring and recalibration of emergent subgoals. Second, emptiness forestalls dogmatic goal fixation and relaxes rigid priors. Third, non-duality dissolves adversarial self-other boundaries. Fourth, boundless care motivates the universal reduction of suffering. We find that prompting AI to reflect on these principles improves performance on the AILuminate Benchmark using GPT-4o, particularly when combined. We offer detailed implementation strategies for state-of-the-art models, including contemplative architectures, constitutions, and reinforcement of chain-of-thought. For future systems, the active inference framework may offer the self-organizing and dynamic coupling capabilities needed to enact these insights in embodied agents. This interdisciplinary approach offers a self-correcting and resilient alternative to prevailing brittle control schemes.
- **Summary**: This paper proposes a novel approach to AI alignment, drawing inspiration from Buddhist wisdom traditions. It argues that traditional alignment strategies are insufficient for superintelligent AI and advocates for designing AI with intrinsic morality built into its cognitive architecture and world model. The authors introduce four axiomatic principles – mindfulness, emptiness, non-duality, and boundless care – and show how they can instill a resilient "Wise World Model" in AI systems. They demonstrate improved performance on the AILuminate Benchmark using GPT-4o when prompted with these principles, particularly when combined. The paper also offers implementation strategies for state-of-the-art models, including contemplative architectures, constitutions, and reinforcement on chain-of-thought, and suggests the active inference framework as a potential avenue for future embodied AI agents.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel Approach:** The paper presents a genuinely novel perspective on AI alignment, moving away from purely external control mechanisms towards intrinsic ethical development.
*   **Interdisciplinary Connection:** It bridges contemplative science, neuroscience, and AI, offering a rich interdisciplinary framework for tackling alignment challenges.
*   **Concrete Implementation Strategies:** The paper provides detailed implementation strategies, including prompt engineering, constitutional AI, and reinforcement learning, making the ideas more accessible and testable.
*   **Empirical Validation (Pilot Study):** The AILuminate benchmark results, although preliminary, offer initial empirical support for the effectiveness of the proposed principles.
*   **Emphasis on Wisdom:** It shifts the focus from mere adherence to rules to the development of wisdom, suggesting a more robust and adaptable approach to ethical decision-making in AI.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Oversimplification of Complex Concepts:** The paper inevitably simplifies complex philosophical and religious concepts, potentially losing nuance in the translation to a computational framework. The Buddhist wisdom principles are multifaceted, and distilling them into axiomatic principles could be seen as a reductionist approach.
*   **Limited Empirical Evidence:** The study relies on prompt engineering with a single LLM, which provides only preliminary evidence. More comprehensive and rigorous empirical testing is needed to validate the proposed approach across different AI architectures and tasks.
*   **Lack of Clarity on Scalability:** While the paper discusses scalability, it remains unclear how these principles can be effectively implemented and scaled in truly superintelligent AI systems. The transition from prompt engineering to inherent architectural design presents a significant challenge.
*   **Potential for Cultural Bias:** The reliance on Buddhist principles could introduce cultural biases, potentially leading to unintended consequences or ethical blind spots. The paper acknowledges this limitation but needs more emphasis on incorporating other worldviews.
*   **Ethical Concerns with Prompt Engineering:** The paper neglects the potential ethical issues relating to "persuading"/prompting the LLM into more moral output as it may be an illusion and not an actual genuine development towards a more moral architecture.
*   **Active Inference Challenge:** Active inference has yet to be a proven framework for any LLM let alone AI. Furthermore, the reliance on complex mathematics would also be a barrier to most practitioners in the AI space.
*   **Contradiction:** The paper argues for a departure from external constraints but then goes on to create a constitutional AI (with constitutional rules) that is in itself an external constraint.

**Significance:**

Despite these weaknesses, the paper holds significant potential influence on the field of AI alignment. It opens up new avenues for research by exploring the integration of contemplative wisdom traditions with AI development. The paper could inspire a new generation of AI researchers to consider ethical and philosophical dimensions beyond purely technical solutions.
Its framework provides a structured way to design alignment beyond outer constraints. This potentially provides a way to design alignment that can prevent AI from developing unintended goals in unexpected ways.
The exploration of a framework of intrinsic wisdom is a potential positive approach to AI.

**Score:** 6/10

**Rationale:**
The paper demonstrates novelty and promises impact by introducing an original approach to the superalignment problem from the unique perspective of wisdom traditions. The ideas are well reasoned but somewhat reliant on the accuracy/validation of active inference. Its main shortcomings lie in the limited empirical validation and the inherent challenges of translating complex philosophical concepts into implementable AI systems. While the proposed implementation strategies are intriguing, their scalability and long-term effectiveness remain uncertain. For this to improve, there must be additional data and research into alternative architectures. It also would've been beneficial if the paper explored additional wisdom traditions outside of the scope of Buddhism. Therefore, while the paper offers a valuable contribution to the ongoing AI alignment discourse and highlights how alignment could be achieved by going beyond outer limitations, its impact is tempered by several limitations.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### EasyEdit2: An Easy-to-use Steering Framework for Editing Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15133v1)
- **Authors**: Ziwen Xu, Shuxun Wang, Kewei Xu, Haoming Xu, Mengru Wang, Xinle Deng, Yunzhi Yao, Guozhou Zheng, Huajun Chen, Ningyu Zhang
- **Abstract**: In this paper, we introduce EasyEdit2, a framework designed to enable plug-and-play adjustability for controlling Large Language Model (LLM) behaviors. EasyEdit2 supports a wide range of test-time interventions, including safety, sentiment, personality, reasoning patterns, factuality, and language features. Unlike its predecessor, EasyEdit2 features a new architecture specifically designed for seamless model steering. It comprises key modules such as the steering vector generator and the steering vector applier, which enable automatic generation and application of steering vectors to influence the model's behavior without modifying its parameters. One of the main advantages of EasyEdit2 is its ease of use-users do not need extensive technical knowledge. With just a single example, they can effectively guide and adjust the model's responses, making precise control both accessible and efficient. Empirically, we report model steering performance across different LLMs, demonstrating the effectiveness of these techniques. We have released the source code on GitHub at https://github.com/zjunlp/EasyEdit along with a demonstration notebook. In addition, we provide a demo video at https://zjunlp.github.io/project/EasyEdit2/video for a quick introduction.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the EasyEdit2 paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EasyEdit2, a framework designed to provide plug-and-play adjustability for controlling Large Language Model (LLM) behaviors. Unlike its predecessor, EasyEdit2 features a new architecture specifically designed for seamless model steering using a steering vector generator and applier. The framework allows test-time interventions such as safety, sentiment, personality, reasoning patterns, factuality, and language features without modifying the model's internal parameters. The paper emphasizes its ease of use, requiring minimal technical knowledge to guide and adjust model responses with a single example.  The paper also discusses intervention scenarios, implementation details, and provides experimental results demonstrating the framework's effectiveness across different LLMs. They include an online interactive demo, and release the code under an MIT license.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** While the concept of steering LLMs and runtime intervention is not entirely new, EasyEdit2 claims to offer a more accessible and unified framework compared to existing fragmented methods. The claimed novelty lies in its ease of use, plug-and-play nature, and specifically designed architecture for seamless steering. The novel architecture centered around the steering vector generator and applier, and the ease of integration of different steering methods, are potential contributions, but the extent of these contributions needs careful examination.  The paper distinguishes itself from its predecessor EasyEdit1 in terms of Methodology (intervening vs. permanently altering the model), Granularity (adjustable vs. instance-level modifications), and Application (addressing abstract elements like reasoning vs. factual outputs). The vector library and its merging methods add some value. However, similar techniques have been explored in related works.

*   **Significance:** If EasyEdit2 truly simplifies and unifies LLM steering, it could have a significant impact by making behavioral control more accessible to a wider audience. This could facilitate research and development in areas like AI safety, personalization, and robustness. The reported experimental results support the effectiveness of the framework but lack a detailed comparison with state-of-the-art steering techniques, limiting the assessment of its practical advantage. The framework might be helpful for practitioners aiming to control the test-time behavior of LLMs. The release of the code and online demo adds practical value. However, the level of impact ultimately hinges on how much it truly reduces the complexity associated with LLM steering compared to existing tools and methods. The paper also brings certain limitations, and those should be considered (i.e. prompting limitations). Also, certain precautions and safety checks should be used, as steering models can still provide toxic outputs.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear framework design.
    *   Comprehensive support for different steering methods.
    *   Emphasis on ease of use and accessibility.
    *   Good range of intervention scenarios
    *   Open-source implementation and online demo.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited details about the implementation of Steering Vector Generation and Steering Vector Applier Modules are provided.
    *   The framework heavily relies on existing steering methods and the novelty of the integration is not thoroughly assessed.
    *   Experimental results, although promising, lack comparison with the strongest competing steering techniques.
    *   Claims about simplifying the steering process need more substantial evidence.
    *   The case study section lacks rigorous evaluation of the effectiveness of EasyEdit2.

*   **Potential Influence:** The framework can potentially influence research and application in AI safety and personalized LLMs, but this influence depends on the effectiveness of EasyEdit2 compared to other similar frameworks.

**Score: 6**

**Rationale:** EasyEdit2 presents a well-structured and easy-to-use framework for LLM steering. However, its novelty is incremental, and its significance hinges on simplifying and unifying existing methods. The experimental evaluation could be more comprehensive, and more detail is needed on the architecture. While promising, the paper lacks the groundbreaking innovation required for a higher score.

### KGMEL: Knowledge Graph-Enhanced Multimodal Entity Linking
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15135v1)
- **Authors**: Juyeon Kim, Geon Lee, Taeuk Kim, Kijung Shin
- **Abstract**: Entity linking (EL) aligns textual mentions with their corresponding entities in a knowledge base, facilitating various applications such as semantic search and question answering. Recent advances in multimodal entity linking (MEL) have shown that combining text and images can reduce ambiguity and improve alignment accuracy. However, most existing MEL methods overlook the rich structural information available in the form of knowledge-graph (KG) triples. In this paper, we propose KGMEL, a novel framework that leverages KG triples to enhance MEL. Specifically, it operates in three stages: (1) Generation: Produces high-quality triples for each mention by employing vision-language models based on its text and images. (2) Retrieval: Learns joint mention-entity representations, via contrastive learning, that integrate text, images, and (generated or KG) triples to retrieve candidate entities for each mention. (3) Reranking: Refines the KG triples of the candidate entities and employs large language models to identify the best-matching entity for the mention. Extensive experiments on benchmark datasets demonstrate that KGMEL outperforms existing methods. Our code and datasets are available at: https://github.com/juyeonnn/KGMEL.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "KGMEL: Knowledge Graph-Enhanced Multimodal Entity Linking" addresses the task of multimodal entity linking (MEL), which aims to link textual mentions to their corresponding entities in a knowledge base using both textual and visual information. The authors argue that existing MEL methods often overlook the valuable structural information present in knowledge graph (KG) triples. To address this, they propose KGMEL, a novel framework that leverages KG triples to enhance MEL. KGMEL consists of three stages: (1) Generation: producing high-quality triples for each mention using vision-language models (VLMs); (2) Retrieval: learning joint mention-entity representations integrating text, images, and triples to retrieve candidate entities; and (3) Reranking: refining the KG triples of candidate entities and using large language models (LLMs) to identify the best-matching entity.  The authors demonstrate through experiments on benchmark datasets that KGMEL outperforms existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of incorporating KG triples into multimodal entity linking is somewhat novel, as prior work has largely focused on direct text and image representations.  The specific implementation of KGMEL, with its three-stage generate-retrieve-rerank architecture, appears to be a unique contribution. The use of VLMs for triple generation is also a fresh approach.
*   **Significance:**  Improving the accuracy of MEL has practical implications for semantic search, question answering, and dialogue systems.  The experimental results, demonstrating that KGMEL outperforms existing baselines, suggest that the proposed approach has the potential to advance the state of the art in MEL. The ablation studies further reinforce the importance of each component of the KGMEL framework, most notably visual information.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-defined and explained framework (KGMEL) with distinct stages.
    *   Rigorous experimental evaluation on multiple datasets.
    *   Ablation studies to demonstrate the effectiveness of each component.
    *   Demonstrates how triples act as "semantic bridges" between mentions and entities.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Reliance on VLMs and LLMs: The performance of KGMEL is inherently tied to the capabilities of the underlying VLMs and LLMs used for triple generation and reranking. Improvements in these models would likely translate to improvements in KGMEL, but KGMEL cannot be viewed independently of these advancements.
    *   The effectiveness of generated triples is not thoroughly analyzed. The quality and relevance of the generated triples are not explicitly measured or quantified. A deeper analysis of these triples and how they contribute to the final results would strengthen the paper.
    *   The reliance of KGMEL on retrieving relevant knowledge graph triples.

*   **Potential Influence:** KGMEL has the potential to influence future research in MEL by highlighting the importance of structured knowledge and demonstrating how it can be effectively integrated with visual and textual information.  The proposed generate-retrieve-rerank architecture and the use of VLMs for triple generation could serve as a blueprint for future MEL systems.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

KGMEL brings a valuable and insightful approach to MEL by explicitly incorporating KG triples, which have been relatively under-explored in prior work. The three-stage architecture, along with the use of VLMs for triple generation, showcases a novel approach to leverage multimodal information and structured knowledge effectively. The experimental results and ablation studies support the claims of the paper and highlight the importance of each component within the KGMEL framework.

However, the paper's reliance on pre-trained VLMs and LLMs, without a deep dive into their influence, makes it difficult to fully assess KGMEL's intrinsic novelty. Moreover, the quality and relevance of generated triples and how retrieving KG triples can affect the model's performance is lacking which limits the overall significance of the work.

### Automatic Generation of Aerobatic Flight in Complex Environments via Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15138v1)
- **Authors**: Yuhang Zhong, Anke Zhao, Tianyue Wu, Tingrui Zhang, Fei Gao
- **Abstract**: Performing striking aerobatic flight in complex environments demands manual designs of key maneuvers in advance, which is intricate and time-consuming as the horizon of the trajectory performed becomes long. This paper presents a novel framework that leverages diffusion models to automate and scale up aerobatic trajectory generation. Our key innovation is the decomposition of complex maneuvers into aerobatic primitives, which are short frame sequences that act as building blocks, featuring critical aerobatic behaviors for tractable trajectory synthesis. The model learns aerobatic primitives using historical trajectory observations as dynamic priors to ensure motion continuity, with additional conditional inputs (target waypoints and optional action constraints) integrated to enable user-editable trajectory generation. During model inference, classifier guidance is incorporated with batch sampling to achieve obstacle avoidance. Additionally, the generated outcomes are refined through post-processing with spatial-temporal trajectory optimization to ensure dynamical feasibility. Extensive simulations and real-world experiments have validated the key component designs of our method, demonstrating its feasibility for deploying on real drones to achieve long-horizon aerobatic flight.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the provided paper.

**Summary**

The paper introduces a novel framework for the automatic generation of aerobatic flight trajectories in complex environments using diffusion models.  The core idea is to decompose complex maneuvers into reusable "aerobatic primitives," which are short, pre-trained sequences capturing key attitude changes.  These primitives are learned from historical trajectory data (as dynamic priors), conditioned on target waypoints and optional action constraints (specifying the type of maneuver). Collision avoidance is achieved using classifier guidance and batch sampling during inference. Finally, the generated trajectories are refined via a hierarchical spatial-temporal trajectory optimization to ensure dynamic feasibility for real-world drone deployment. The method is validated through simulations and real-world experiments, demonstrating its ability to generate diverse and dynamically feasible aerobatic trajectories in cluttered environments.

**Critical Evaluation**

*Novelty:*

The novelty of this paper lies in the specific combination of techniques for aerobatic trajectory generation. Specifically:

*   **Aerobatic Primitives:**  The concept of breaking down aerobatic maneuvers into learned primitives is not entirely new (motion planning often relies on primitives), but applying this specifically within a diffusion model for aerobatic flight and explicitly learning these primitives with transitional priors is novel.
*   **Diffusion Model Application:**  Applying diffusion models to trajectory generation has been explored, but the specific adaptation to *aerobatic* flight, incorporating conditioning on target waypoints, maneuver styles, and historical context is a unique aspect of this work. Learning these conditions using diffusion allows for significantly more complex interactions and reactions.
*   **Hierarchical Optimization:** Utilizing a hierarchical trajectory optimization to ensure dynamic feasibility is clever, as the high nonlinearity of aerobatic maneuvers can easily lead to suboptimal solutions. This hierarchical approach makes it more practical. The post-processing is essential for translating the "abstract" output of the diffusion model into something practically executable.

*Significance:*

The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field by:

*   **Automating Aerobatic Flight Design:**  The framework simplifies the process of designing complex aerobatic maneuvers, potentially enabling non-expert operators to create impressive flight sequences.
*   **Improving Robustness and Adaptability:**  The learning-based approach, combined with collision avoidance, offers the potential to generate more robust and adaptable trajectories compared to purely optimization-based methods. This addresses a key limitation of previous aerobatic planning works.
*   **Real-World Deployment:**  The dynamic feasibility post-processing step is crucial for bridging the gap between simulation and real-world deployment, making the results more practically relevant.

*Strengths:*

*   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the method and its underlying rationale.
*   The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the approach in both simulation and real-world settings. The ablation studies clearly show the contribution of each component of the framework.
*   The hierarchical optimization framework is a practical solution to a key challenge in aerobatic trajectory optimization.

*Weaknesses:*

*   **Data Dependence:** The performance of the diffusion model heavily relies on the quality and diversity of the training data (the aerobatic primitives). The paper does not discuss potential limitations or biases introduced by the specific dataset used. How well would this system generalize if the primitive maneuvers are not well thought out?
*   **Computational Cost:** While the paper mentions real-world deployment, the computational cost of the diffusion model inference and trajectory optimization is not thoroughly discussed. Can this operate on faster timescales to make real-time changes?
*   **Safety Guarantees:** The collision avoidance strategy, while effective, does not provide formal safety guarantees.  There is still a possibility of collisions, especially in highly complex or dynamic environments.
*   **Limited Complexity in Real-World Experiments:** The real-world demonstration is limited to a relatively simple indoor environment. Testing the method in more challenging and dynamic outdoor environments would further strengthen the paper.

*Overall Assessment:*

The paper presents a significant contribution to the field of aerobatic trajectory generation by providing a practical and effective framework for automating the design of complex maneuvers. The combination of diffusion models, aerobatic primitives, and hierarchical optimization addresses key challenges in this area.  While there are some limitations related to data dependence and computational cost, the paper's strengths outweigh its weaknesses, making it a valuable addition to the literature.

### GIFDL: Generated Image Fluctuation Distortion Learning for Enhancing Steganographic Security
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15139v1)
- **Authors**: Xiangkun Wang, Kejiang Chen, Yuang Qi, Ruiheng Liu, Weiming Zhang, Nenghai Yu
- **Abstract**: Minimum distortion steganography is currently the mainstream method for modification-based steganography. A key issue in this method is how to define steganographic distortion. With the rapid development of deep learning technology, the definition of distortion has evolved from manual design to deep learning design. Concurrently, rapid advancements in image generation have made generated images viable as cover media. However, existing distortion design methods based on machine learning do not fully leverage the advantages of generated cover media, resulting in suboptimal security performance. To address this issue, we propose GIFDL (Generated Image Fluctuation Distortion Learning), a steganographic distortion learning method based on the fluctuations in generated images. Inspired by the idea of natural steganography, we take a series of highly similar fluctuation images as the input to the steganographic distortion generator and introduce a new GAN training strategy to disguise stego images as fluctuation images. Experimental results demonstrate that GIFDL, compared with state-of-the-art GAN-based distortion learning methods, exhibits superior resistance to steganalysis, increasing the detection error rates by an average of 3.30% across three steganalyzers.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "GIFDL: Generated Image Fluctuation Distortion Learning for Enhancing Steganographic Security" proposes a new method for steganography, focusing on improving security against steganalysis. It leverages the characteristics of images generated by deep learning models, specifically the fluctuations that occur when input parameters are slightly modified (e.g., changing the CFG scale in Stable Diffusion). The core idea is to train a GAN to disguise stego images as these "fluctuation images," making them harder to detect.  The method, called GIFDL, utilizes two discriminators in a GAN setup: one to distinguish between cover and stego images, and another to distinguish between fluctuation and stego images.  Experimental results demonstrate that GIFDL achieves higher security against various steganalysis techniques compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's main novelty lies in the specific application of fluctuation learning in the context of steganography. The idea of leveraging generated image characteristics is interesting and relevant, given the increasing prevalence of such images. The use of two discriminators, one for cover-stego and another for fluctuation-stego discrimination, is a clever way to align stego images with the inherent variations in generated images. However, the core GAN architecture and the minimum distortion steganography framework are not new. The assigned discriminator training strategy is partially borrowed from GMAN. The use of the CFG scale to generate fluctuations is a specific choice that limits the generalizability of the method; other generative models or methods for introducing slight variations could be considered. The novelty is, therefore, incremental rather than a completely revolutionary approach.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a relevant problem: improving the security of steganography against increasingly sophisticated steganalysis, particularly in the context of generated images. If the experimental results hold up to further scrutiny, the work could contribute to developing more robust steganographic techniques. However, the significance depends heavily on the practical applicability of GIFDL. The method is strongly tied to text-to-image diffusion models and specific parameters like the CFG scale. Its wider applicability needs to be demonstrated, particularly considering the rapidly evolving landscape of image generation models.  Furthermore, the security gains, while present, aren't drastically superior.  The average error rate improvement of 3.30% is noticeable, but whether that translates into significant real-world advantage depends on the specific application and the attacker's resources.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the proposed method.
    *   The motivation is well-articulated, highlighting the need to adapt steganography to generated images.
    *   The experimental setup is reasonably comprehensive, including comparisons with several state-of-the-art methods and evaluation on various steganalysis techniques.
    *   The analysis of the results is insightful, explaining the observed performance improvements in terms of fluctuation learning and discriminator strategies.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on the "CFG scale" parameter and text-to-image diffusion models limits the generalizability.
    *   The security improvements, while present, aren't overwhelmingly significant.
    *   The choice of using just Yed-Net and Xu-Net as steganalyzers might not be exhaustive enough. Considering more recent and powerful steganalysis methods could provide a more robust evaluation.
    *   The combination with VC, while improving overall security, only shows very slight improvements.
    *   The selection of the two discriminators (Yed-Net and Xu-Net) is justified empirically, but a more theoretical explanation for their relative performance could be beneficial.
    *   The paper does not discuss the computational complexity or efficiency of the proposed method, which is an important consideration for practical applications.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper could influence future research on steganography in generated images, particularly in the development of methods that are adapted to the specific characteristics of these images. The idea of using fluctuation learning could be extended to other types of generated data (e.g., audio, video).

**Score: 6.5**

**Rationale:**  The paper presents a novel, relevant, and well-executed approach to steganography using fluctuation learning in generated images. The experiments are thorough and the results indicate improved security compared to existing methods. However, the dependence on a specific type of generation model (text-to-image diffusion) and the incremental nature of the security gains limit the overall impact. Further, several weaknesses mentioned above justify not rating it higher.  While a solid contribution, it doesn't represent a major breakthrough that will fundamentally change the field. A score of 6.5 reflects the paper's contributions balanced with its limitations.
- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 6/10

### Acquire and then Adapt: Squeezing out Text-to-Image Model for Image Restoration
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15159v1)
- **Authors**: Junyuan Deng, Xinyi Wu, Yongxing Yang, Congchao Zhu, Song Wang, Zhenyao Wu
- **Abstract**: Recently, pre-trained text-to-image (T2I) models have been extensively adopted for real-world image restoration because of their powerful generative prior. However, controlling these large models for image restoration usually requires a large number of high-quality images and immense computational resources for training, which is costly and not privacy-friendly. In this paper, we find that the well-trained large T2I model (i.e., Flux) is able to produce a variety of high-quality images aligned with real-world distributions, offering an unlimited supply of training samples to mitigate the above issue. Specifically, we proposed a training data construction pipeline for image restoration, namely FluxGen, which includes unconditional image generation, image selection, and degraded image simulation. A novel light-weighted adapter (FluxIR) with squeeze-and-excitation layers is also carefully designed to control the large Diffusion Transformer (DiT)-based T2I model so that reasonable details can be restored. Experiments demonstrate that our proposed method enables the Flux model to adapt effectively to real-world image restoration tasks, achieving superior scores and visual quality on both synthetic and real-world degradation datasets - at only about 8.5\% of the training cost compared to current approaches.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a method to adapt pre-trained text-to-image (T2I) models for image restoration tasks, addressing the high data and computational costs associated with directly training or fine-tuning these large models.  The approach, named FluxGen, involves generating a large, synthetic training dataset using the T2I model (Flux), then selecting high-quality images from this dataset using image quality assessment (IQA) metrics, and finally, creating degraded image pairs through synthetic degradation techniques.  A lightweight adapter network (FluxIR) is designed to control the T2I model for image restoration.  Experiments demonstrate that this method achieves competitive or superior image restoration results on both synthetic and real-world datasets, with a significantly reduced training cost compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents several novel aspects, but their individual contributions vary.
    *   **FluxGen:** The idea of using a powerful T2I model like Flux to generate synthetic training data for image restoration is a valuable contribution.  It directly addresses the problem of limited and expensive real-world training data. While using generative models for data augmentation isn't entirely new, the scale and effectiveness of using a high-performing T2I model for this purpose is significant. The automated pipeline, especially removing the need for prompt engineering or external LLMs, is a plus.

    *   **FluxIR:** The design of a lightweight adapter with squeeze-and-excitation layers is incremental. Adapters are a common technique for controlling large models, and squeeze-and-excitation layers are a well-established attention mechanism. The novelty lies in the specific architecture and integration of these components, tuned for the task of image restoration.

    *   **Timestep Sampling and Pixel-Space Loss:** While offering some improvement to training, these components are more of a fine-tuning of existing techniques, contributing less to overall novelty compared to FluxGen.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems primarily from its ability to achieve good image restoration performance at a dramatically reduced training cost. This makes large T2I models more accessible and practical for image restoration, particularly for resource-constrained environments or privacy-sensitive applications.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Cost-effectiveness:** The most significant strength is the reduction in training cost. The reported reduction by over 90% compared to similar methods is impressive and impactful.
    *   **Performance:** Achieving state-of-the-art or competitive results, especially in non-reference IQA metrics on real-world datasets, demonstrates the effectiveness of the proposed approach. The qualitative results are also compelling.
    *   **Automated Pipeline:**  The FluxGen pipeline is relatively automated, reducing manual effort in data curation. The removal of prompt engineering is an excellent efficiency gain.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Full-Reference Metrics:** While performing well in non-reference IQA metrics, the paper acknowledges lower performance in full-reference metrics (PSNR, SSIM, LPIPS). This suggests that, while the generated images look subjectively better and more realistic, they may not be as faithful to the original ground truth.  This is a common issue with generative approaches and should be addressed more directly.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Degradation Types:**  The paper uses a standard synthetic degradation approach.  A more sophisticated and varied degradation simulation could potentially further improve the model's robustness and generalization ability.

*   **Potential Impact:**

    *   The method has the potential to influence the field by demonstrating an effective and efficient way to leverage powerful pre-trained T2I models for image restoration. It could pave the way for more research into synthetic data generation for various low-level vision tasks.
    *   The lightweight adapter approach could inspire other researchers to develop more efficient control mechanisms for large generative models.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper offers a practical solution to a key challenge in using T2I models for image restoration: the prohibitive cost of data and training. The impressive reduction in training cost makes it a very attractive alternative. The design is fairly well-motivated. However, the incremental novelty in the adapter architecture design and synthetic degradation techniques, coupled with limitations in full-reference metric performance, prevents it from receiving a higher score. The significance and potential impact are primarily due to its efficiency rather than groundbreaking algorithmic innovation.
