### The Synthetic Imputation Approach: Generating Optimal Synthetic Texts For Underrepresented Categories In Supervised Classification Tasks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15160v1)
- **Authors**: Joan C. Timoneda
- **Abstract**: Encoder-decoder Large Language Models (LLMs), such as BERT and RoBERTa, require that all categories in an annotation task be sufficiently represented in the training data for optimal performance. However, it is often difficult to find sufficient examples for all categories in a task when building a high-quality training set. In this article, I describe this problem and propose a solution, the synthetic imputation approach. Leveraging a generative LLM (GPT-4o), this approach generates synthetic texts based on careful prompting and five original examples drawn randomly with replacement from the sample. This approach ensures that new synthetic texts are sufficiently different from the original texts to reduce overfitting, but retain the underlying substantive meaning of the examples to maximize out-of-sample performance. With 75 original examples or more, synthetic imputation's performance is on par with a full sample of original texts, and overfitting remains low, predictable and correctable with 50 original samples. The synthetic imputation approach provides a novel role for generative LLMs in research and allows applied researchers to balance their datasets for best performance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a "synthetic imputation" approach to address the problem of underrepresented categories in supervised classification tasks using Large Language Models (LLMs). The core idea is to leverage a generative LLM (specifically GPT-40) to create synthetic text examples for these underrepresented categories.  The method involves carefully crafting prompts and providing a small number of original examples to guide the LLM in generating synthetic data that is both similar in meaning and sufficiently different to avoid overfitting. The paper presents empirical results demonstrating that this approach can achieve performance comparable to using the full dataset, even with a limited number of original examples, and that it outperforms a state-of-the-art data augmentation technique (SSMBA) in terms of reducing overfitting.  The author argues that this method makes data augmentation more accessible to researchers, particularly in fields like political science where access to large, balanced datasets can be limited.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its specific application of generative LLMs for synthetic data augmentation in the context of supervised classification, emphasizing the balance between similarity and diversity in the generated data. While data augmentation and using generative models for data creation are not entirely new concepts, the paper provides a specific and practical method with an emphasis on prompting strategies and a comparison against a state-of-the-art (but pre-generative LLM era) technique. The explicit focus on underrepresented categories and the demonstration of reduced overfitting is also a valuable contribution. Prior methods often focused on overall dataset augmentation without directly addressing class imbalance.
* **Significance:** The significance of this work rests on its potential to democratize data augmentation for researchers with limited resources. The ease of implementation (using readily available LLMs and a relatively simple prompting strategy) makes it accessible to a wider audience than more complex data augmentation algorithms. Furthermore, the reduction in overfitting compared to previous methods, and the predictable nature of the minimal overfitting that does occur, makes this a valuable and robust contribution.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly defines the problem of underrepresented categories in supervised learning and its impact on model performance.
    * **Practical Methodology:** The synthetic imputation approach is well-defined, and the paper provides practical guidelines for prompt engineering.
    * **Empirical Validation:** The paper includes empirical results on two different datasets, demonstrating the effectiveness of the approach and comparing it to a baseline and a state-of-the-art alternative.
    * **Accessibility:** The method utilizes accessible LLMs.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on a Specific LLM:**  The paper heavily relies on GPT-40. While other LLMs are mentioned as possibilities, the experiments are not replicated across different LLM architectures.  The prompt engineering may need adjustments depending on the LLM used.
    * **Limited Generalizability Assessment:** While the paper argues for generalizability to other classification tasks, the empirical evaluation is limited to two specific datasets.
    * **Supervision of text generation:** The approach still requires a researcher to supervise and evaluate the text generated which can be a time and resource-intensive bottleneck.

**Justification for Score:**

While the individual components of this research are not groundbreaking on their own, the combination of generative LLMs for data augmentation, with a specific focus on addressing underrepresented classes and minimizing overfitting, makes this a useful and significant addition to the field. The accessibility of the method to a broader range of researchers in different disciplines is a crucial advantage. The empirical validation, while limited, provides convincing evidence of the approach's effectiveness.

I would rate this paper as a **7** due to its novelty, practical utility, and clear demonstration of benefits. While there are weaknesses in terms of reliance on a specific LLM and limited generalizability, the paper offers a valuable and accessible solution to a common problem in supervised learning, especially in contexts where data is scarce.

### DSPO: Direct Semantic Preference Optimization for Real-World Image Super-Resolution
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15176v1)
- **Authors**: Miaomiao Cai, Simiao Li, Wei Li, Xudong Huang, Hanting Chen, Jie Hu, Yunhe Wang
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in diffusion models have improved Real-World Image Super-Resolution (Real-ISR), but existing methods lack human feedback integration, risking misalignment with human preference and may leading to artifacts, hallucinations and harmful content generation. To this end, we are the first to introduce human preference alignment into Real-ISR, a technique that has been successfully applied in Large Language Models and Text-to-Image tasks to effectively enhance the alignment of generated outputs with human preferences. Specifically, we introduce Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) into Real-ISR to achieve alignment, where DPO serves as a general alignment technique that directly learns from the human preference dataset. Nevertheless, unlike high-level tasks, the pixel-level reconstruction objectives of Real-ISR are difficult to reconcile with the image-level preferences of DPO, which can lead to the DPO being overly sensitive to local anomalies, leading to reduced generation quality. To resolve this dichotomy, we propose Direct Semantic Preference Optimization (DSPO) to align instance-level human preferences by incorporating semantic guidance, which is through two strategies: (a) semantic instance alignment strategy, implementing instance-level alignment to ensure fine-grained perceptual consistency, and (b) user description feedback strategy, mitigating hallucinations through semantic textual feedback on instance-level images. As a plug-and-play solution, DSPO proves highly effective in both one-step and multi-step SR frameworks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of misalignment between human perception and the output of Real-World Image Super-Resolution (Real-ISR) models.  It introduces Direct Semantic Preference Optimization (DSPO), a method that incorporates human preference alignment into Real-ISR. DSPO leverages semantic guidance at the instance level to improve alignment, by addressing limitations in directly applying techniques like Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) from LLMs. The method involves two key strategies: (1) Semantic instance alignment, which aims for fine-grained perceptual consistency, and (2) User description feedback, which utilizes semantic textual feedback on instance-level images to mitigate hallucinations. The paper claims DSPO is a plug-and-play solution effective in both one-step and multi-step SR frameworks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The primary novelty lies in the introduction of human preference alignment into Real-ISR, drawing inspiration from techniques used in LLMs and T2I.  While DPO itself is not new, its adaptation and the development of DSPO specifically tailored to the challenges of Real-ISR are novel.  The two-pronged semantic guidance approach, with instance alignment and user feedback, also contributes to the novelty.  The paper appears to be the first to systematically address human preferences in Real-ISR using a DPO-like framework.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance is tied to the increasing importance of perceptual quality in SR models.  While many SR models focus on quantitative metrics (PSNR, SSIM), the paper tackles the subjective aspect of human preference. Hallucination and artifact generation are persistent problems, and the proposed approach directly aims to address them.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Problem Definition:** Clearly identifies and articulates the limitations of existing Real-ISR methods.
    *   **Methodological Approach:** The design of DSPO, with its two-pronged semantic guidance approach, is well-motivated. Addressing limitations and adapting DPO makes sense.
    *   **Empirical Evaluation:** The paper reports results on real-world datasets (RealSR, DRealSR) along with a synthetic dataset, which helps validate the effectiveness of the approach. The user study is also a strong point. Ablation studies are included, providing some insight into the contribution of the separate elements.
    *   **Clarity:** Overall the paper is written clearly and organized well.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Complexity:** The reliance on SAM for semantic segmentation adds a dependency. While SAM is a powerful tool, its use may introduce its own biases or limitations that the paper doesn't fully address. The impact of errors or uncertainties in the instance segmentation on the downstream preference learning isn't discussed in detail.
    *   **User Feedback Incorporation:** The precise methodology for collecting and incorporating user feedback is somewhat vague. The selection process for hallucinated instances using similarity metrics is a bit simplistic, and a discussion of more robust methods would be beneficial.
    *   **Generalizability:**  The paper doesn't discuss the generalizability of the user descriptions for different types of images.
    *   **Impact Measurement:** The impact of DSPO is primarily demonstrated through preference ratings, which can be subjective. While these are important, it would be valuable to see if the method leads to tangible benefits in downstream tasks.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by encouraging researchers to consider human perceptual quality more explicitly in SR model design. DSPO provides a concrete framework for incorporating human preferences. The methods is relatively simple to integrate as well given that the framework uses open-source models.

**Overall Justification:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by explicitly addressing the human preference aspect in Real-ISR.  The methodological approach is well-designed, addressing specific challenges with DPO in this context.  However, the reliance on SAM, the user feedback incorporation process, and the evaluation of generalizability present some limitations.  The study of different strategies also helps justify the importance of each component.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### FaceCraft4D: Animated 3D Facial Avatar Generation from a Single Image
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15179v1)
- **Authors**: Fei Yin, Mallikarjun B R, Chun-Han Yao, Rafał Mantiuk, Varun Jampani
- **Abstract**: We present a novel framework for generating high-quality, animatable 4D avatar from a single image. While recent advances have shown promising results in 4D avatar creation, existing methods either require extensive multiview data or struggle with shape accuracy and identity consistency. To address these limitations, we propose a comprehensive system that leverages shape, image, and video priors to create full-view, animatable avatars. Our approach first obtains initial coarse shape through 3D-GAN inversion. Then, it enhances multiview textures using depth-guided warping signals for cross-view consistency with the help of the image diffusion model. To handle expression animation, we incorporate a video prior with synchronized driving signals across viewpoints. We further introduce a Consistent-Inconsistent training to effectively handle data inconsistencies during 4D reconstruction. Experimental results demonstrate that our method achieves superior quality compared to the prior art, while maintaining consistency across different viewpoints and expressions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "FaceCraft4D: Animated 3D Facial Avatar Generation from a Single Image" presents a novel framework for creating high-quality, animatable 3D facial avatars from a single input image. The method leverages shape, image, and video priors to overcome the inherent limitations of single-image reconstruction. It begins with 3D-GAN inversion for coarse shape estimation, enhances textures using depth-guided warping and an image diffusion model for cross-view consistency, and incorporates a video prior for expression animation.  A key contribution is a "Consistent-Inconsistent" (COIN) training strategy designed to handle data inconsistencies during the 4D reconstruction process. The authors demonstrate superior quality compared to existing methods, maintaining consistency across viewpoints and expressions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper combines several existing techniques (3D GAN inversion, diffusion models, video priors, Gaussian splatting, and FLAME model) in a novel way.  The primary novel contribution appears to be the COIN training strategy, designed to improve robustness to inconsistencies in the generated multi-view data. While individual components are not new, the combination and the COIN training method represent a unique pipeline for single-image 4D avatar generation.  The warping-based control generation signal also appears to be a novel improvement.

* **Significance:** Generating high-quality, animatable 3D avatars from a single image has significant potential impact across gaming, education, and film. Reducing the reliance on multi-view data makes avatar creation more accessible.  The results demonstrate improved quality and consistency compared to prior art, showcasing practical advancements. The real-time rendering capability provided by Gaussian splatting further enhances the practical value.  However, the improvements, while noticeable, are incremental rather than revolutionary. There has been significant progress in this field over the last few years.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Comprehensive pipeline:**  The framework addresses the multiple challenges of single-image avatar generation (shape, texture, animation, consistency) through a well-designed, modular approach.
    * **COIN training:** This training strategy is a significant contribution, addressing a key weakness in many similar methods.
    * **Good results:**  Qualitative and quantitative results demonstrate improvements over existing methods, especially in consistency and quality.
    * **Real-time rendering:** Leveraging Gaussian splatting allows for real-time rendering performance.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on existing components:**  The core of the method relies heavily on pre-trained models (3D GAN, diffusion model, FLAME), so the novelty is primarily in integration and the COIN training strategy.
    * **Incremental improvement:**  While the results are better, the improvement in visual quality over state-of-the-art methods doesn't appear dramatically significant. This suggests some limitations in one or more components that still need to be refined.
    * **Limited evaluation dataset:**  The method relies on comparing against other methods using non-reference metrics since ground truth data is difficult to obtain, making a thorough evaluation challenging.

* **Potential Impact:** The paper provides a solid advancement in the field of single-image avatar generation. The COIN training and combined use of image, shape, and video priors is a good approach to achieving consistent performance. This could influence future research directions in dealing with data inconsistencies for 3D generative models. Broader adoption will depend on further improvements in the realism of the generated avatars.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7**. The paper presents a novel combination of existing techniques with a novel component (COIN Training) to improve the state of the art for a relevant problem. The improvements in visual quality and consistency, while not revolutionary, are clearly demonstrated. This combination is able to create very consistent 3D faces from 2D images. However, the reliance on several pre-trained models and incremental nature of the quality improvements limit the overall impact.
